  Long-video understanding with multimodal LLMs is fundamentally constrained by the mismatch between video length and the model's limited visual context budget, making frame selection essential for practical reasoning. Existing query-aware methods typically rely on heuristic pipelines: they assign one-dimensional query-conditioned scores to frames and then apply sampling, ranking, or extraction rules to form the final subset. While effective in practice, such methods are generally not derived from an explicit optimization objective and struggle to capture the heterogeneous evidence required for complex reasoning. In this paper, we revisit long-video frame selection from a principled optimization perspective. We formulate it as a structured evidence-allocation problem and propose OTFS, an optimal transport framework that maps multiple evidence sources onto video frames. To reflect the asymmetry of this problem---important evidence should be preserved, whereas redundant frames need not absorb mass---we instantiate this view with a semi-unbalanced entropic optimal transport objective and an efficient solver, OTFS-Sinkhorn. Combined with an exact dynamic program for coverage-aware subset extraction, OTFS yields a practical two-stage training-free pipeline. We further show that, in the single-source case, our formulation induces a Gibbs-type distribution over frames, making Q-Frame's temperature-scaled distribution a special case of our framework while also providing a broader perspective for understanding related prior methods. Experiments on three long-video understanding benchmarks show that OTFS consistently outperforms strong training-free baselines. Codes are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[NeoWorld-3: From Pixels to Code-Defined Physical Worlds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27690)**  `arXiv:2610.27690`  `cs.CV`  
  _Eachen Soong, Zijian Niu, Haoran Xu, Yihong Dong, Yunbo Wang, Xiaokang Yang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Simulation-ready world reconstruction demands physical interactivity beyond visual realism, which existing black-box 3D generation fails to deliver. We present NeoWorld-3, a coding-agent framework that reconstructs interactive 3D worlds from visual observations via tunable, executable programs. At its core, NeoSDK provides the underlying computational engine for low-level geometries, part composition, differentiable parameter refinement, and physics validation. At the same time, NeoMCP exposes these capabilities as structured, tool-callable APIs for vision-language agents to visually inspect, edit code, and iteratively refine programs. Through a hierarchical coordinator-worker architecture, the framework scales seamlessly from standalone articulated objects to complex compositional scenes. Evaluations across object-level and scene-level reconstruction benchmarks demonstrate that NeoWorld-3 achieves state-of-the-art geometric fidelity and physical readiness. Crucially, manipulation trajectories planned within our reconstructed twins achieve successful zero-shot execution on a physical dual-arm robot, completing the Real-Sim-Real loop.
  </details>

- **[WaveSpec: Multi-Spectral Iris Presentation Attack Detection via Wavelength-Conditioned Spectral Coherence Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27608)**  `arXiv:2610.27608`  `cs.CV`  
  _Raghavendra Ramachandra_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multispectral iris presentation attack detection benefits from observing the same iris across multiple near-infrared wavelengths, but existing methods often use wavelength information mainly for conditioning or fusion. This can limit their ability to explicitly model whether the spectral response of a sample is coherent across bands. In this paper, we propose \textbf{WaveSpec}, a multispectral iris PAD framework that performs feature-level spectral coherence adaptation. Each NIR band is first encoded with a DINOv2 backbone. The proposed Set Conditioned Spectral Coherence Adapter then updates each band feature using the consensus of the available spectral set, the band-specific deviation from that consensus, and wavelength identity. The adapted features are projected into structural, spectral, and anomaly representations, adaptively pooled, and classified as bona fide or attack. We evaluate WaveSpec on the MSIrPAD benchmark under a strict cross-artefact protocol, where the model is trained with one presentation attack instrument and tested on unseen attacks. WaveSpec reduces the average D-EER from 8.45\% for SpectraIrisPAD to 3.33\%, and reduces average BPCER@APCER=1\% from 43.16\% to 12.07\%. Ablation results show that the SCSA adapter and spectral residual are central to the improvement, while a band-subset analysis shows that WaveSpec remains effective under partial spectral observations and performs best when all five bands are available. The method adds only modest computational overhead, with 11.15 ms end-to-end latency per five-band sample on an NVIDIA L40S-48Q GPU.
  </details>

- **[DART: Scaling a Shape Representation Across Anatomical Classes and Imaging Modalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27605)**  `arXiv:2610.27605`  `cs.CV`  
  _Koushik Swarna, Pedro R. A. S. Bassi, Lam Ngo, Wenxuan Li, Jiancheng Yang, Alan Yuille, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Current state-of-the-art segmentation models still produce anatomically implausible shapes, such as broken vessels and fragmented glands, on the structures that are hardest to segment. Learned shape representations capture what structures look like and can help correct these errors, but most cover a fixed set of anatomical classes. Extending this coverage across classes and imaging modalities is costly because it often requires new voxel-level annotations. We propose Diffusion for Anatomical Reshaping with Text (DART), which learns a shared shape representation from segmentation probabilities. It uses anatomical descriptions to correct these probabilities without receiving medical images. Across 13 public benchmarks, absolute Dice gains reach 31% for individual gallbladder cases in CT and 10% for individual prostate cases in MRI. Using existing segmentation predictions, we scale a single shape representation from 50 to 377 anatomical classes without new voxel-level annotations for correction training. The shared representation also corrects four lesion classes absent from training and transfers from CT to MRI without retraining. Corrected labels also improve segmentation models, raising one model's Dice from 57.1% to 67.5%. The same pipeline supports annotations for 45,000 CT scans. These results suggest that sharing shape information within and across classes can reduce the need for new voxel-level annotations when extending segmentation to more anatomical classes and imaging modalities. The model is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[HiQGS: Hierarchical Spatiotemporal Querying for Feed-Forward Gaussian Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27604)**  `arXiv:2610.27604`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qiaowei Miao, Qifeng Cheng, Yijing Guo, Yuru Xiao, Ziqian Ni, Sheng Yang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Feed-forward Gaussian reconstruction from sparse posed monocular frames requires reasoning over observations that vary jointly in viewpoint and time. Existing methods either predict Gaussian primitives from observation-aligned features or aggregate multi-frame information through compact latent queries. However, the former tightly couples Gaussian allocation to individual frames, while the latter does not explicitly structure observed evidence according to its temporal relation to the requested timestamp. Consequently, evidence across spatial scales, viewpoints, and timestamps can be difficult to align and integrate. We present \textbf{HiQGS}, a query-based hierarchical framework for feed-forward Gaussian scene reconstruction from sparse monocular frames. HiQGS introduces a \textbf{Hierarchical Spatiotemporal Encoder} that organizes multi-resolution geometry-appearance visual features according to image location, observation time, and camera pose, thereby preserving complementary appearance and structural cues. Our \textbf{Time-Adaptive Decoder} use target timestamp queries the resulting hierarchical representation to retrieve relevant spatiotemporal evidence and predicts the corresponding Gaussian scene state. Experiments on static and dynamic scene reconstruction benchmarks demonstrate strong rendering quality and geometric accuracy, validating the effectiveness of hierarchical visual encoding and target-time-aware Gaussian querying.
  </details>

- **[Causal Spatiotemporal State Evolution for Monocular 4D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27594)**  `arXiv:2610.27594`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qiaowei Miao, Yijing Guo, Lipei Zhang, Qifeng Cheng, Yuru Xiao, Ziqian Ni, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While feed-forward models excel in static 3D geometry estimation, extending them to dynamic 4D scenes requires modeling visual geometry as a causally evolving state. Current methods typically rely on unordered temporal aggregation, which lacks the directional structure of physical time. Furthermore, existing supervision often treats geometry and motion as disjoint objectives, failing to exploit their inherent physical coupling. Consequently, they lack a unified constraint to effectively supervise temporal state transitions, leading to inconsistent 4D scene representations. To address this, we propose a feed-forward framework for causal 4D visual geometry learning from monocular videos. At its core, Causal Spatiotemporal Mamba augments temporal aggregation with directional state propagation. Through complementary Temporal-Spatial and Spatial-Temporal Scans, CSM continuously propagates geometry-aware states across frames to model spatiotemporal evolution. To align this causal state evolution with observable dynamics, we introduce Unified Motion Supervision. Rather than treating motion as an independent objective, UMS leverages geometry-aware predictions from the camera and depth heads to construct a unified forward motion field that is consistent with the underlying 3D structure. This geometry-grounded supervision explicitly constrains inter-frame state transitions, enabling stable states propagation across frames. Extensive evaluations on dynamic benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across video depth, camera pose, and optical flow estimation. These results validate that the synergy between the directional state propagation of Causal Spatiotemporal Mamba and Unified Motion Supervision effectively captures continuous scene evolution, significantly reducing temporal drift and reinforcing spatial-temporal consistency in 4D representation learning.
  </details>

- **[Real In, Real Out: What If We Only Use Real Data for Scene Text Editing?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27592)**  `arXiv:2610.27592`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xingsong Ye, Yongkun Du, Jiaxin Zhang, Chong Sun, Zhineng Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Aiming to modify textual content while preserving the original style, Scene Text Editing (STE) has significant practical value in many applications. STE typically requires paired images before and after editing for training. However, recent approaches rely heavily on synthetic paired data and complex disentanglement strategies. They struggle to faithfully capture the diversity and complexity of natural scenes, often producing artifacts or hallucinations. In this work, we challenge the common practice and investigate whether STE can be driven by turning each real sample into its own paired supervision. Concretely, we explore multiple ways to disrupt the original image (e.g., Crop, Shuffle, Mask) to construct self-paired training data, and build a strong baseline (TextRIRO}) upon a conditional diffusion architecture. Our experiments show that combining character-column shuffling with image cropping (Shuffle&Crop) effectively destroys text semantics while preserving key style cues, enabling the model to learn editing behaviors directly from the real text image. With this concise yet real-oriented design, TextRIRO achieves promising performance in both accuracy and visual realism across real-world benchmarks, substantially mitigating the synthetic-to-real domain gap. Furthermore, we leverage TextRIRO to build TextRIRO-3M, a large-scale realistic dataset for long-text recognition by concatenating edited and original images. Experimental results show that training recognition models with TextRIRO-3M significantly improves recognition accuracy, demonstrating that TextRIRO provides highly useful training resources for downstream tasks. Overall, TextRIRO lifts STE to realistic, reliable, and widely applicable new levels. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Look Before You Reason: Implicit Visual Thinking for Efficient Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27341)**  `arXiv:2610.27341`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zerui Chen, Changrui Chen, Fei Ni, Jiankang Deng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language models (VLMs) have advanced rapidly in multimodal reasoning, yet they remain limited on tasks requiring fine-grained understanding of small or easily overlooked image regions. Recent thinking with images approaches mitigate this limitation by allowing models to inspect images through operations such as zooming, cropping, or code execution. While effective, their multi-turn interactions with VLMs introduce redundant token computation and slow inference, and separate region inspection from the reasoning step that depends on it. To address these issues, we propose Look Before You Reason (LoRe), an implicit visual thinking framework for efficient visual reasoning within a single-turn VLM interaction. Given an image and a question, LoRe predicts the most relevant image region, aligns it with the corresponding image patch features, and strengthens the associated key-value (KV) cache entries, enabling subsequent decoding to directly exploit the selected visual cues without additional visual inputs or explicit tool calls. We further employ reinforcement learning to guide the VLM toward identifying informative image regions and leveraging them during reasoning. To support fine-grained evaluation of multimodal reasoning, we introduce COCO-ObjQA, a large-scale benchmark for object-centric multimodal reasoning. Experiments on three challenging multimodal benchmarks demonstrate that LoRe consistently improves VLM reasoning performance with low additional inference overhead.
  </details>

- **[LVMem: Image-Augmented Memory for Long-Horizon Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27104)**  `arXiv:2610.27104`  `cs.CV`  
  _Victoria Khoruzhaya, Maksim Kalmykov, Tuong Do Minh, Tatiana Zemskova, Yixin Zhu, Dmitry Yudin_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reliable language-guided perception is essential for embodied agents, where questions about objects, events, and their locations must be answered from long observation histories. Unlike conventional visual question answering, realistic robotic settings require reasoning over extended temporal horizons and persistent scene memory. We build upon the view-based memory paradigm and propose an efficient framework for long-horizon spatio-temporal question answering. While existing view-based systems typically store either text captions or visual embeddings, we extend the ReMEmbR-style memory with image paths that link each stored description to its original visual content. To make the stored annotations more informative, we replace uniform sampling during memory construction with an optical-flow-based frame selection strategy (PSFR) and store an object-centric descriptive scene representation instead of plain captions. We further equip a VLM-based agent with an adaptive retrieval tool that returns the image associated with the most relevant caption, allowing the agent to decide when visual inspection is needed. On the OC-NaVQA benchmark, LVMem outperforms ReMEmbR on binary and positional metrics with both backbones and matches or exceeds a 4D scene graph DAAAM baseline on binary accuracy, while operating on a lightweight view-based memory that requires no explicit graph construction.
  </details>

- **[BUMP: Body-driven Unified Multi-object Physics for HOI Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27066)**  `arXiv:2610.27066`  `cs.CV`  
  _Wonjung Woo, Hyeonwoo Kim, Hanbyul Joo_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generating human-object interactions (HOIs) with multiple objects is challenging because the resulting motions arise from different underlying causes. Human motion reflects semantic intent, intentionally manipulated objects must remain coordinated with the human, while surrounding objects move primarily as physical consequences of contact and collision. We present BUMP, a text-driven framework for multi-object HOI generation based on a simple principle: learn intentional motion and simulate physical consequences. Our method first generates full-body human motion from text and scene context, then predicts trajectories for a small number of intentionally manipulated driver objects conditioned on the human motion. We then obtain responder object motions through physics simulation, allowing the framework to handle varying numbers and configurations of surrounding objects without expanding the learned trajectory output space. To address the scarcity of paired full-body multi-object HOI data, we combine heterogeneous supervision from human-only motion, full-body HOI, and hand-centric manipulation data augmented with plausible body motion. We further introduce outcome-conditioned interaction editing, which infers a local modification of the causal driver object trajectory from a desired responder object behavior without requiring an explicit target driver object trajectory. Experiments demonstrate strong performance on both single-object and multi-object HOI benchmarks. Qualitative results showcase diverse scenarios involving inter-object relationships and edits, highlighting the advantage of our unified model formulation.
  </details>

- **[Deep Auxiliary Learning via Bilevel Feature Optimization for Label-Efficient Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26954)**  `arXiv:2610.26954`  `cs.CV`  
  _Fuping Wu, Xi Jia, Le Zhang, Jiahua Li, Rima Mustafa, Thomas E. Nichols, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Auxiliary learning (AL), which can improve the performance of a primary task by leveraging related auxiliary tasks, is commonly regarded as a special case of multi-task learning. Existing AL approaches typically require manually annotated auxiliary labels, increasing annotation costs, particularly in medical imaging where expert labeling is expensive. In this work, we investigate whether segmentation performance can be improved using auxiliary tasks whose labels are automatically derived from existing segmentation annotations, requiring no additional manual labeling. To address this, we propose a novel auxiliary learning framework, formulated as a bilevel optimization problem. Our method enables effective knowledge transfer between tasks via a feature communication module, enhancing the primary segmentation task. We evaluate the method on three medical image segmentation benchmarks under limited-label settings. The results demonstrate consistent improvements over state-of-the-art methods, achieving superior label efficiency. The code is publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SuperClip Pyramid for Video Temporal Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26925)**  `arXiv:2610.26925`  `cs.CV`  
  _Sanghyeok Lee, Juyeon Ko, Joonmyung Choi, Jongha Kim, Dohwan Ko, Ji Soo Lee, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  With the rapid expansion of online video content, video temporal grounding has become essential for localizing moments based on user-specific queries, comprising two subtasks: moment retrieval (MR) and highlight detection (HD). While recent approaches adopt DETR-style decoders or temporal pyramid architectures, they rely on single-scale feature maps or uniformly downsampled representations, limiting their ability to capture semantic boundaries and variable-length events. In this paper, we present SuperClip Video Temporal Grounding (SCVTG) built on a multi-scale temporal hierarchy that better captures distinctive events at various granularities. SCVTG constructs a SuperClip pyramid by grouping adjacent clips into semantically coherent segments, termed SuperClips. Further, to aggregate these SuperClips having variable duration and temporal location, we introduce a temporally adaptive kernel that aggregates SuperClips through temporally conditioned weights, enabling flexible aggregation. On top of this, we design relative saliency losses to guide the model in learning inter-clip importance, enhancing its ability to distinguish relevant events. Extensive experiments show that SCVTG achieves the best performance across five video temporal grounding benchmarks with promising gains.
  </details>

- **[Controllable Multi-Object Insertion for Detection Data Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26922)**  `arXiv:2610.26922`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiyang Huang, Tianwei Zhang, Xiaoqin Zhang, Ling Shao, Shijian Lu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Controllable object insertion provides a targeted approach to augmenting object detection datasets that require category--count fidelity, annotation alignment, and source-content preservation beyond visual realism. Several approaches have been proposed to address this grand challenge, but most suffer from different constraints in precise category--count requests and visual consistency, limited coordination across categories and instances, and poor localization of multiple objects of specific category and count. We design SceneP2F, a two-stage trainable framework that connects scene-aware joint planning with layout-aligned generation leveraging explicit instance-level layouts. SceneP2F consists of two novel designs. The first is ScenePlan which allocates one class-labeled query per requested instance, jointly predicts scene-compatible object centers, and separately estimates box sizes conditioned on the planned centers and scene context. The second is SceneAlign-Fill which combines geometry- and appearance-aware conditioning, box--class-guided regional attention, and an annotation-region-weighted flow-matching objective to render the planned objects while limiting unintended changes to source content. Extensive experiments on three challenging benchmarks UAVDT, VisDrone, and BDD100K show that SceneP2F outperforms representative general-purpose editors and object-insertion methods consistently, demonstrating the value of coordinating instance-level planning and generation for category-targeted detection augmentation.
  </details>

- **[Memory Internalization via Test-Time Training for Long-Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26910)**  `arXiv:2610.26910`  `cs.CV`  
  _Haonan Zhao, Jiaxuan Chen, Qingyang Liu, Shuochen Chang, Siyu Sun, Jiangtong Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Long-video understanding often requires answering many questions about a single video whose evidence is distributed across extended temporal contexts. Conventional video-language models repeatedly encode frames or retrieve visual evidence for every question. Parameterized video memory offers a write-once alternative, but existing methods rely primarily on synthesized text and fixed adaptation configurations, leaving open where video knowledge should be written and how visual evidence should shape the memory. We introduce Memory Internalization via Test-Time Training (MIT^3), a per-video adaptation framework that writes each long video into compact, video-specific adaptive parameters for video-free, text-only question answering. Under compact multi-scale supervision, causal tracing measures the causal memory utility of each model layer for video-grounded answering and selects video-specific adaptation locations. Visual memory distillation then trains the text-only memory to reproduce the representation change induced by the relevant video clip, preserving the internal effect of visual evidence beyond textual supervision. Across LVBench, LongVideoBench, and Video-MME, MIT^3 achieves data-efficient video-free question answering with substantially fewer synthesized samples than prior parameterized video-memory methods, demonstrating that effective video internalization depends on both where knowledge is written and how visual evidence is distilled into the model.
  </details>

- **[PnP-Transformer: A Neural Pose Solver for Zero-Shot 6D Object Pose Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26096)**  `arXiv:2610.26096`  `cs.CV`  
  _Emirkan Burak Yilmaz, Yakup Genc_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present PnP-Transformer, a neural pose solver designed as an iterative-free, differentiable alternative to classical PnP-RANSAC algorithms. PnP-Transformer regresses the 6D object pose by processing predicted semi-dense 2D-3D correspondence fields alongside confidence scores, while mapping geometric inputs into a continuous latent space. To evaluate its practical utility, we integrate PnP-Transformer into a zero-shot 6D pose estimation pipeline, where the correspondence module and pose solver are trained separately. PnP-Transformer achieves a 41.2% mean AR score with low latency (0.46 s/obj) on seven core BOP benchmark datasets, surpassing all prior baselines (such as RayPose with 39.1% AR) but one (Co-op with 58.4% AR). When the pose solver is replaced with EPnP-RANSAC (similar to Co-op) while keeping the correspondence module unchanged, the RANSAC alternative outperforms PnP-Transformer on six of the seven datasets by reaching 52.2% mean AR at a cost of an additional 60 ms per object, revealing an accuracy-latency trade-off. Code and pretrained models are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Risk Decomposition for Hallucination-Aware Video Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26069)**  `arXiv:2610.26069`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chenyan Zheng, Fan Wu, Ruibo Li, Jiaxing Li, Minghao Yang, Bang Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Despite achieving high visual fidelity, video models still suffer from physical hallucinations, limiting their reliability as world simulators. Existing methods leverage model-internal or external signals to improve physical plausibility, but their roles and limitations require a unified explanation. We propose a two-dimensional physical-hallucination risk framework that defines low-density sampling risk and high-density drifting risk. The first risk captures generations assigned low likelihood by the video model, for example, a ball suddenly teleporting despite the model favoring continuous motion, while the second risk captures physical inconsistencies in high-likelihood generations, for example, a ball bouncing progressively higher without additional energy. Our analysis explains how model-internal signals steer sampling toward higher-density regions of the learned distribution to address low-density sampling risk. However, higher model likelihood does not guarantee physical plausibility, motivating external guidance to address high-density drifting risk. Based on this framework, we develop Hallucination-Aware Sampler (HA-Sampler), which applies external physical guidance within latent refinement to generate physically plausible videos, and HA-Scorer, which derives a likelihood-based signal from the generator itself for evaluating generated videos using continuous normalizing flow (CNF), with coarse ODE integration and finite-difference divergence estimates. Experiments on Physics-IQ and VideoPhy benchmarks support our theoretical analysis and demonstrate that our methods outperform the compared sampling and scoring SOTA baselines.
  </details>

- **[LEVI: Benchmarking Visual Identity Fidelity with Verifiable Low-Entropy Constructs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25716)**  `arXiv:2610.25716`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiao Ma, Zhonghao Zhan, Hamed Haddadi, Qi Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Modern generative models can produce visually plausible images while violating exact identity constraints. Evaluating such failures requires distinguishing violations of the requested identity from permissible variation in appearance. We introduce Low-Entropy Visual Identity (LEVI), which characterizes visual identities determined by compact facts and explicit rules. The low-entropy requirement concerns identity uncertainty, while appearance may remain diverse. We formulate identity fidelity in terms of the probability that a conditional image distribution assigns to the identities permitted by the prompt. Visual Identity Spaces (VIS) formalize this distinction by specifying admissible identity values and which different realizations express the same identity. Building on this formulation, we develop Verifiable Visual Grammar (VVG), a declarative language whose typed primitives and compositional constraints describe identity-bearing structures. Using this grammar, we build a benchmark for image generation. It assesses each output against the task's identity requirements, drawing evidence from the final pixels. The curated benchmark contains 1,375 tasks spanning 120 base identity spaces across six domains. Among ten evaluated model configurations, the highest whole-task pass rate is 58.69%. These results reveal a persistent gap between visually plausible images and faithful realization of their specified identities. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[PCXR-Copilot: Device-aware pediatric chest radiographic analysis with segmentation, suppression, and edema-related cues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25486)**  `arXiv:2610.25486`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xueyang Li, Alec Girten, Peng Zeng, Mingze Jiang, Shaoyang Huang, Gelei Xu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Patients admitted to pediatric ICU and cardiac ICU often require intensive perioperative support and frequent chest radiography to monitor support devices, extravascular lung water, and body-wall edema. Radiographic interpretation and analysis is often difficult due to superimposed anatomic and iatrogenic structures that obscure clinically relevant signals. While deep learning can provide decision support, translation to pediatric radiographs is still limited by high device burden and scarcity of pediatric datasets with clinically grounded device annotations. To address this gap, we first introduce PediaDeviceSeg-1K, a curated dataset of 1,000 anterior-posterior radiographs of pediatric CICU patients with clinically grounded, fine-grained annotations of common ICU devices. To our best knowledge, this is the first publicly available pediatric CICU CXR resource with clinically grounded device annotations for device-focused benchmarking. Building on PediaDeviceSeg-1K, we further present PCXR-Copilot, a device-aware pipeline that outputs lung and device masks, optional device-suppressed views, and edema-related visualization maps and scalar indices. The full pipeline has been integrated and runs in real time within the TWIST platform at Boston Children's Hospital, demonstrating feasibility for real-world clinical deployment.
  </details>

- **[ShadowCast: On-the-Fly Context-Aware Shadow Augmentation for Remote Sensing Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23440)**  `arXiv:2610.23440`  `cs.CV`  
  _Dongho Yoon, Yechan Kim, Jongmin Joo, JongHyun Park, Moongu Jeon_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Shadows cast by elevated objects alter the spectral appearance and obscure the boundaries of objects in remote sensing images, thereby degrading semantic segmentation performance. Most existing approaches handle shadows as degradations to be detected and removed, requiring additional costs from dedicated networks and shadow-specific annotations. We propose ShadowCast, which takes the opposite approach: it synthesizes shadows and injects them during training for remote sensing segmentation. It extracts masks of elevated objects, such as buildings and trees, directly from the segmentation labels and estimates an image-specific cast-shadow direction cue by probing the brightness around them. It then projects synthetic shadows along the inferred cast-shadow direction, requiring no learned network and no acquisition metadata. Experiments on three widely used remote sensing benchmarks, Vaihingen, Potsdam, and LoveDA, show that ShadowCast consistently outperforms the baseline across diverse state-of-the-art segmentation architectures. Moreover, it brings additional performance gains when combined with standard augmentation and regularization techniques, indicating that stochastic shadow injection serves as a simple and complementary augmentation strategy for remote sensing segmentation.
  </details>

- **[PURE: Refining Feed-forward 3D Reconstruction with Unlabeled Post-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14698)**  `arXiv:2610.14698`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yaolong Zhu, Peng Li, Jiahao Lu, Chengfeng Zhao, Mengfei Li, Chao Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models have achieved remarkable progress through large-scale training and strong learned priors. However, they remain fragile when applied to dynamic in-the-wild videos, where non-rigid motion, occlusions, and illumination changes are prevalent. A key limitation lies in their reliance on expensive depth and camera pose annotations, which hinders scaling to diverse real-world dynamic data and restricts generalization. To address this issue, we propose \textbf{PURE}, an unlabeled post-training refinement framework that improves depth and camera estimation without requiring ground-truth supervision. Instead of relying on annotations, PURE elegantly distills robust structural knowledge from state-of-the-art 2D priors into 4D feed-forward architectures. PURE leverages relative depth constraints, affine-invariant geometry distillation, and pose refinement based on feature correspondences to stabilize geometric predictions under challenging dynamic conditions. Extensive experiments on both synthetic and real-world dynamic benchmarks demonstrate that PURE delivers consistent improvements across multiple state-of-the-art architectures. Furthermore, our data-efficient paradigm effectively mitigates domain shifts, successfully unlocking the architectural limits of these base models in challenging, in-the-wild dynamic scenes.
  </details>

- **[GPU-Accelerated Computation of Persistent Homology for Topological Analysis of Image Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10959)**  `arXiv:2610.10959`  `cs.CV`  
  _Fan Wang, Hubert Wagner, Rezaul Chowdhury, Chao Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In recent years, persistent homology has seen rapid adoption in deep learning, yet its computation remains a major bottleneck in network training. This paper introduces TopoGPU, a GPU streaming pipeline that computes persistence diagrams of cubical complexes induced by 2D and 3D images. TopoGPU streams the input image chunk by chunk, processing each chunk with massively parallel GPU kernels on a grid of GPU blocks; the resulting boundary relations are accumulated in host memory, where the CPU performs the boundary matrix reduction. TopoGPU introduces a stratification-aware discrete Morse matching that provably preserves persistent homology under streaming, together with a parallel topological sorting algorithm and a parallel V-path parity algorithm for deriving Morse boundaries on the GPU. TopoGPU outperforms Cubical Ripser, a state-of-the-art method for persistent homology computation, on every benchmark evaluated, achieving an average end-to-end speedup of 53.24x and a maximum of 198.01x. We further integrate TopoGPU into a topology-preserving deep network, demonstrating that it substantially reduces the cost of persistent homology computation during network training. TopoGPU is open source, with pre-built binaries, Google Colab notebooks, and Docker images available at the project's GitHub page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[HarnessIR: Harnessing Multimodal Foundation Models for Universal Real-World Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10133)**  `arXiv:2610.10133`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiangtao Kong, Shuaizheng Liu, Rongyuan Wu, Lingchen Sun, Zhengqiang Zhang, Jixin Zhao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Real-world low-quality images suffer from complex mixed degradations, including but not limited to noise, blur, atmospheric effects, etc. Recent agentic methods usually model real-world image restoration (Real-IR) as a sequential tool calling problem over task-specific single-degradation restoration models. This paradigm, however, is fundamentally limited because complex real-world degradations cannot be cleanly undone degradation by degradation, and the tool used for task-specific models caps the capability of the agent system. In this work, we present HarnessIR, an agentic framework for Real-IR by harnessing a multimodal foundation model (MFM) as the executor. HarnessIR consists of five stages: perception and diagnosis, on-demand tool invocation, prompt composition, execution, and verification-driven refinement. Unlike prior agentic Real-IR methods that rely on tool chains assembled from task-specific models, HarnessIR feeds the restoration requirements, the perceptual diagnosis, and the evidence into an MFM that performs restoration in a single pass, followed by verification stages to determine whether the result warrants further processing. Under our harness, off-the-shelf MFMs handle restoration tasks remarkably well, achieving state-of-the-art results on the widely used MiO100 synthetic benchmark. More importantly, by exploiting the strong generalization ability of MFMs, HarnessIR delivers compelling restoration quality on challenging real-world scenes where previous agentic IR systems often struggle. Codes is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[DriveVLA-M0: Failure-Aware Memory Augmentation for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10413)**  `arXiv:2608.10413`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zebin Xing, Yupeng Zheng, Qiang Chen, Linbo Wang, Yichen Zhang, Pengxuan Yang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have recently emerged as a promising paradigm for end-to-end autonomous driving by enabling unified reasoning across perception, language, and planning. However, existing approaches lack mechanisms to exploit past failures or adapt to distribution shifts, causing the model to persistently underperform on similar scenarios where it has previously failed. In this paper, we propose DriveVLA-M0, a retrieval-augmented VLA with failure-aware latent memory. We construct a latent memory pool that stores failure cases along with their structure scene representations and expert trajectory labels, and design a dedicated Retrieve Model that decouples static road structure and dynamic agent interactions to enable structurally grounded retrieval. At inference time, retrieved cases are injected into the model via a lightweight decoupled LoRA-based test-time training (TTT) mechanism, allowing targeted and scenario-specific correction without modifying the backbone. Extensive experiments on NAVSIMv1 and NAVSIMv2 benchmark demonstrate that our approach consistently outperforms prior methods, achieving 94.1 PDMS on Navtest and 47.0 EPDMS on Navhard with only 26.44 ms TTT backward latency overhead. Furthermore, we show that DriveVLA-M0 scales effectively with additional memory, enabling training-free performance gains through memory expansion. The code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[LaP-Forensics: Latent-Pixel Consistency Guided Multimodal Reasoning for Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25962)**  `arXiv:2607.25962`  `cs.CV`  
  _Can Wang, Yuhao Wang, Yushe Cao, Canran Xiao, Fei Shen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent generative models can produce images with few obvious visual artifacts, weakening detectors and explanations that rely only on surface appearance. We present LaP-Forensics, a multimodal framework that augments RGB semantics with reconstruction-based forensic evidence. A frozen Stable Diffusion DDIM inversion-reconstruction model provides a fixed reconstruction reference, and its residual map measures local compatibility with that reference. Independent projectors encode the RGB image and residual map before a structured Where-What-Why model predicts a textual analysis and an artifact mask. Supervised fine-tuning is followed by Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), whose reward combines mask overlap with output-structure and evidence-reference terms. These text-side terms encourage the model to refer to the consistency map but do not constitute a verifier of free-form textual truth. A separate image-level head fuses RGB and DDIM-residual class features. Experiments show cross-generator detection on UniversalFakeDetect and competitive artifact localization on the official SynthScars benchmark. Controlled cue-construction, inversion-horizon, component, reward-term, and counterfactual analyses support the utility of the residual stream under the evaluated settings, while free-form textual faithfulness and reliability under post-processing remain open limitations.
  </details>

- **[TAMF-VTON: Token-Wise Sparse Adaptation for Mask-Free Virtual Try-On](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14807)**  `arXiv:2607.14807`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jie Wang, Qian He, Gaofeng He, Xiaogang Jin, Huamin Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion-based virtual try-on (VTON) often relies on segmentation-derived masks that become unreliable when the reference garment differs substantially from the target person's clothing. Existing methods may also lose fine details and commonly focus on single-garment transfer. We present TAMF-VTON, an inference-time mask-free framework built on Qwen-Image-Edit-2509. Given a person image, one or more garment images, and a text instruction, it performs single- or multi-garment transfer without masks, parsing maps, pose representations, or spatial controls at inference. Its central component is token-wise sparse Mixture-of-Experts adaptation, which provides input-dependent capacity without predefined expert roles. Timestep-weighted frequency supervision regularizes high-frequency reconstruction, while adaptive offline data construction supplies cross-category and cross-silhouette training tuples; neither is used at inference. TAMF-VTON achieves the best scores on most reported paired and unpaired metrics among evaluated VTON baselines on the standard single-garment VITON-HD and DressCode benchmarks. Selected qualitative results illustrate multi-garment composition, subject-to-subject transfer, diverse scenes, body-shape variation, and illumination changes. With INT4 on an RTX 4090, TAMF-VTON generates 1K-resolution images under 15 seconds and 2K-resolution images under 30 seconds. Our project can be accessed viathis https URL.
  </details>

- **[MolmoMotion: Forecasting Point Trajectories in 3D with Language Instruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18558)**  `arXiv:2606.18558`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jianing Zhang, Chenhao Zheng, Yajun Yang, Max Argus, Rustin Soraki, Winson Han, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Motion forecasting is central to visual intelligence: agents must anticipate how objects will move in order to plan actions, reason about physical interactions, and synthesize realistic futures. We argue that 3D points in world coordinates provide a general representation that is class-agnostic, view-stable, compact, and directly useful for downstream tasks. We formalize the task of goal-conditioned 3D point motion forecasting: given a short visual history, a set of 3D query points on an object of interest, and a language description of the intended goal, the model predicts the future 3D trajectory of each point. We introduce a full stack to study this task at scale: (1) MolmoMotion-1M is a large corpus of action-described, object-grounded 3D point trajectories annotated from 1.16M unconstrained videos; (2) PointMotionBench is a human-verified benchmark spanning 111 object categories and 61 motion types; and (3) MolmoMotion is a general motion forecasting model that supports both autoregressive coordinate prediction and flow-matching-based trajectory generation. MolmoMotion accurately predicts diverse motion patterns with different language instructions, and significantly outperforms existing motion prediction baselines on PointMotionBench. Finally, we show that the learned 3D motion prior transfers well to downstream applications: it improves training efficiency and generalization for robot manipulation, and its predicted trajectories provide effective motion guidance for generative models to synthesize videos with more realistic object motion.
  </details>

- **[LADBench: A Benchmark for Logical Fault Detection in Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17433)**  `arXiv:2606.17433`  `cs.CV`  
  _Sahasra Kondapalli, Lara Radovanovic, Aadi Palnitkar, Mingyang Mao, Xiaomin Lin_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large Vision Language Models (VLMs) excel at visual question answering and semantic grounding, but their capacity for autonomous logical reasoning remains underexplored. Existing anomaly benchmarks emphasize visual errors or direct prompting rather than the physical and social common sense needed for open-world deployment. To address this, we introduce LAD-bench, a benchmark of more than 1,000 curated synthetic images with logical anomalies across four domains: Residential, Urban, Collaborative, and Nature. We further propose a Tiered Prompting Protocol based on progressive disclosure, which measures how much explicit assistance a model needs to localize and reason about a logical fault. Evaluating leading foundation models reveals substantial weaknesses: even the best achieves only 70.11% overall accuracy, showing that implicit logical fault detection remains unsolved. Crucially, models often fail to identify anomalies even after receiving explicit hints in deeper tiers. By surfacing these limitations in sequential multimodal reasoning, LAD-Bench offers a rigorous framework for advancing the safety, reliability, and cognitive alignment of autonomous visual systems. Dataset and Code:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Efficient One-Step Diffusion Restoration Model with Compact Token Compression and Linear Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23451)**  `arXiv:2605.23451`  `cs.CV`  
  _Bingtian Qiao, Yue Shi, Yingjie Zhou, Yong Guo, Guangtao Zhai, Jiezhang Cao_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Real-world image super-resolution aims to recover high-quality images from complex and unknown real-world degradations. However, existing generative Real-ISR methods largely inherit the dense latent representations and quadratic-cost global modeling paradigm developed for high-resolution image synthesis, causing computation, memory usage, and inference latency to scale unfavorably with resolution and thus limiting practical deployment. We argue that the key bottleneck lies not in insufficient restoration priors, but in excessive token redundancy and costly token interactions during high-resolution restoration. Motivated by this observation, we revisit Real-ISR from the perspectives of compact latent representation and linear-complexity modeling, and propose SANA-SR, an efficient one-step restoration framework. Specifically, SANA-SR employs a deep compression autoencoder with a 32x compression ratio to drastically reduce latent tokens while preserving restoration-relevant structures and textures. On top of this compact latent space, we introduce a linear-attention DiT with LoRA fine-tuning, enabling efficient high-resolution restoration with linear-complexity token mixing. Extensive experiments on all benchmark datasets demonstrate that SANA-SR achieves highly competitive and often superior quantitative performance against existing methods, while restoring clearer and more realistic textures. Moreover, after pruning, the deployed model runs in 0.019s with 407.95G MACs and 344M parameters, highlighting its strong potential for practical mobile deployment.
  </details>

- **[IndusAgent: Agentic Reinforcement Learning for Cross-Category Industrial Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20682)**  `arXiv:2605.20682`  `cs.CV`  
  _Rongbin Tan, Fangfang Lin, Jinguo Luo, Min Qiu, Kejin Cui, Mengmeng Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Small defects in unfamiliar product categories can be difficult to distinguish from benign appearance variations in a global view. Additional reasoning over the same view may leave this ambiguity unresolved. We introduce IndusAgent, a Qwen3-VL-8B inspection policy trained through supervised fine-tuning and tool-augmented reinforcement learning for cross-category industrial anomaly detection. The policy selects among six tools for cropping, enhancement, measurement, within-image comparison, texture analysis, and external visual opinions from Qwen3-VL-Max, then integrates returned observations into a structured diagnosis. Indus-CoT provides 3,000 trajectories from 26 Real-IAD categories, generated with actual tool feedback and screened against annotations after teacher generation. GRPO optimizes an accuracy-gated objective that conditions localization, type, and tool rewards, including call costs, on final diagnostic correctness while retaining an independent format reward. Across five benchmarks, IndusAgent achieves 83.5% mean balanced accuracy, compared with 79.4% for direct Max inference under the common protocol. Across three training seeds, the five-benchmark mean balanced accuracy is 80.5\% for a weight-matched ungated control and 83.5% for the gated policy. Evaluation uses disjoint product categories and a fixed defect vocabulary.
  </details>

- **[Only Train Once: Uncertainty-Aware One-Class Learning for Face Authenticity Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10040)**  `arXiv:2605.10040`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qingchao Jiang, Zhenxuan Hou, Zhiying Zhu, Zhenxing Qian, Xinpeng Zhang, Zaiwang Gu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The rapid evolution of generative paradigms has enabled the creation of highly realistic imagery, which escalates the risks of identity fraud and the dissemination of disinformation. Most existing approaches frame face forgery detection as a fully supervised binary classification problem. Consequently, these models typically exhibit significant performance decay when tasked with detecting forgeries from previously unseen generative paradigms. Furthermore, these methods focus exclusively on either DeepFakes or fully synthesized faces, thereby failing to provide a generalized framework for universal face forgery detection. In this paper, we address this challenge by introducing FADNet (Face Authenticity Detector Net), which reformulates face forgery detection as a one-class classification (OCC) task. By training exclusively on authentic facial data to capture their intrinsic representations, FADNet flags any image whose feature embedding deviates significantly from the learned distribution of real faces as a forgery. In this way, FADNet requires only a single training process without retraining for each specific forgery method. The framework incorporates Evidential Deep Learning (EDL) to quantify predictive uncertainty and utilizes a plug-and-play pseudo-forgery image generator (PFIG) to tighten decision boundaries around authentic data. Extensive experimental evaluations on the DF40 and ASFD benchmarks demonstrate that FADNet achieves superior performance and generalization capabilities. Specifically, FADNet substantially outperforms existing state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods, yielding a remarkable average accuracy of 96.63\% and an average precision of 98.83\%. Our code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[DMDSC: A Dynamic-Margin Deep Simplex Classifier for Open-Set Recognition on Medical Image Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00675)**  `arXiv:2605.00675`  `cs.CV`  
  _Vishal, Arnav Aditya, Nitin Kumar, Saurabh J. Shigwan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Medical imaging datasets are often characterized by extreme class imbalances, where rare pathologies are significantly underrepresented compared to common conditions. This imbalance poses a dual challenge for Open-Set Recognition (OSR): models must maintain high classification accuracy on known classes while reliably rejecting unknown samples unseen during training in the clinical settings. While recently proposed Deep Simplex Classifier (DSC)~\cite{cevikalp2024reaching} and UnCertainty-aware Deep Simplex Classifier (UCDSC)~\cite{Aditya_2026_WACV} successfully leverage Neural Collapse to ensure maximal inter-class separation, they rely on a uniform margin that does not account for the varying densities of medical classes.In this paper, we propose DMDSC an enhanced framework featuring a dynamic margin approach. Our approach automatically adapts class-specific margins based on label frequency, enforcing a higher penalty and tighter feature clustering for rare pathologies to counteract the effects of data imbalance. Extensive experiments conducted on diverse medical benchmarks on BloodMNIST\cite{medmnistv2}, OCTMNIST\cite{medmnistv2}, DermaMNIST\cite{medmnistv2}, and BreaKHis~\cite{spanhol2015dataset} datasets, demonstrate that our framework outperforms state-of-the-art methods.
  </details>

- **[HERMES++: Toward a Unified Driving World Model for 3D Scene Understanding and Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.28196)**  `arXiv:2604.28196`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xin Zhou, Dingkang Liang, Xiwu Chen, Feiyang Tan, Dingyuan Zhang, Hengshuang Zhao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Driving world models serve as a pivotal technology for autonomous driving by simulating environmental dynamics. However, existing approaches predominantly focus on future scene generation, often overlooking comprehensive 3D scene understanding. Conversely, while Large Language Models (LLMs) demonstrate impressive reasoning capabilities, they lack the capacity to predict future geometric evolution, creating a significant disparity between semantic interpretation and physical simulation. To bridge this gap, we propose HERMES++, a unified driving world model that integrates 3D scene understanding and future geometry prediction within a single framework. Our approach addresses the distinct requirements of these tasks through synergistic designs. First, a BEV representation consolidates multi-view spatial information into a structure compatible with LLMs. Second, we introduce LLM-enhanced world queries to facilitate knowledge transfer from the understanding branch. Third, a Current-to-Future Link is designed to bridge the temporal gap, conditioning geometric evolution on semantic context. Finally, to enforce structural integrity, we employ a Joint Geometric Optimization strategy that integrates explicit geometric constraints with implicit latent regularization to align internal representations with geometry-aware priors. Extensive evaluations on multiple benchmarks validate the effectiveness of our method. HERMES++ achieves strong performance, outperforming specialist approaches in both future point cloud prediction and 3D scene understanding tasks. The model and code will be publicly released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SARES-DEIM: Sparse Mixture-of-Experts Meets DETR for Clutter-Aware SAR Ship Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.04127)**  `arXiv:2604.04127`  `cs.CV`  
  _Fenghao Song, Shaojing Yang, Xi Zhou_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Ship detection in Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) imagery is challenged by coherent speckle noise, complex coastal clutter, and the prevalence of small-scale targets. Conventional detectors, primarily designed for optical imagery, often exhibit limited adaptability to SAR-specific degradation and suffer from the loss of fine-grained ship signatures during spatial downsampling. To address these limitations, we propose SARES-DEIM, a domain-aware detection framework grounded in the DEtection TRansformer (DETR) paradigm. Central to our approach is SARESMoE (SAR-aware Expert Selection Mixture-of-Experts), a module leveraging a sparse gating mechanism to selectively route features toward specialized wavelet, spatial, and frequency-oriented experts. This sparsely activated architecture enhances clutter-aware representation while keeping the active inference cost controlled. Furthermore, we introduce the Space-to-Depth Enhancement Pyramid (SDEP) neck to preserve high-resolution spatial cues from shallow stages and improve the localization of small targets. Experiments on HRSID and SAR-Ship-Dataset show that SARES-DEIM achieves competitive results across both public benchmarks, with more prominent advantages on the challenging HRSID dataset. Specifically, SARES-DEIM achieves 76.4% mAP50:95 and 93.8% mAP50 on HRSID, while keeping the active computation at 72.6 GFLOPs and 136.5 FPS on a single NVIDIA A40 GPU with batch size 8; the batch-size-1 latency is 37.6 ms.
  </details>

- **[TrajTok: Learning Trajectory Tokens enables better Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22779)**  `arXiv:2602.22779`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chenhao Zheng, Jieyu Zhang, Jianing Zhang, Weikai Huang, Ashutosh Kumar, Quan Kong, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Tokenization in video models, typically through patchification, generates an excessive and redundant number of tokens. This severely limits video efficiency and scalability. While recent trajectory-based tokenizers offer a promising solution by decoupling video duration from token count, they rely on complex external segmentation and tracking pipelines that are slow and task-agnostic. We propose TrajTok, an end-to-end video tokenizer module that is fully integrated and co-trained with video models for a downstream objective, dynamically adapting its token granularity to semantic complexity, independent of video duration. TrajTok contains a unified segmenter that performs implicit clustering over pixels in both space and time to directly produce object trajectories in a single forward pass. By prioritizing downstream adaptability over pixel-perfect segmentation fidelity, TrajTok is lightweight and efficient, yet empirically improves video understanding performance. With TrajTok, we implement a video CLIP model trained from scratch (TrajViT2). It achieves the best accuracy at scale across both classification and retrieval benchmarks, while maintaining efficiency comparable to the best token-merging methods. TrajTok also proves to be a versatile component beyond its role as a tokenizer. We show that it can be seamlessly integrated as either a probing head for pretrained visual features (TrajAdapter) or an alignment connector in vision-language models (TrajVLM) with especially strong performance in long-video reasoning.
  </details>

- **[Frequency-Domain Decomposition and Recomposition for Robust Audio-Visual Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.18912)**  `arXiv:2509.18912`  `cs.CV`  
  _Kai Peng, Yunzhe Shen, Miao Zhang, Jia Wang, Leiye Liu, Wei Ji, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Audio-visual segmentation (AVS) plays a critical role in multimodal machine learning by effectively integrating audio and visual cues to precisely segment objects or regions within visual scenes. Recent AVS methods have demonstrated significant improvements. However, they overlook the inherent frequency-domain contradictions between audio and visual modalities--the substantial noise often present in audio high-frequency signals vs. the structurally rich details in visual high-frequency signals. Ignoring these differences can result in suboptimal performance. In this paper, we rethink the AVS task from a deeper perspective by reformulating AVS task as a frequency-domain decomposition and recomposition problem. To this end, we introduce a novel Frequency-Aware Audio-Visual Segmentation (FAVS) framework consisting of two key modules: Frequency-Domain Enhanced Decomposer (FDED) module and Synergistic Cross-Modal Consistency (SCMC) module. FDED module employs a residual-based iterative frequency decomposition to discriminate modality-specific semantics and structural features, and SCMC module leverages a mixture-of-experts architecture to reinforce semantic consistency and modality-specific feature preservation through dynamic expert routing. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our FAVS framework achieves state-of-the-art performance on three benchmark datasets, and abundant qualitative visualizations further verify the effectiveness of the proposed FDED and SCMC modules. The project repository is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[ArtSeek: Deep artwork understanding via multimodal in-context reasoning and late interaction retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.21917)**  `arXiv:2507.21917`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nicola Fanelli, Gennaro Vessio, Giovanna Castellano_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Analyzing digitized artworks requires both visual interpretation and deep artistic, contextual, and historical knowledge. We introduce ArtSeek, a multimodal framework for art analysis that combines multimodal large language models with retrieval-augmented generation. Unlike prior work, our pipeline relies only on image input, so it applies to artworks without links to Wikidata or Wikipedia, common in most digitized collections. ArtSeek integrates three components: a late-interaction multimodal retrieval module, a contrastive multitask network predicting artist, genre, style, media, and tags, and an agentic reasoning strategy enabled through in-context examples. Central to this approach is WikiFragments, a dataset of image-text fragments extracted from the entire English Wikipedia to support knowledge-grounded multimodal reasoning. Our multimodal query encoding raises retrieval NDCG@5 from 33.9 to 44.9 over encoding the full image-text query, and fixed-budget token pooling keeps late-interaction search practical over millions of fragments at under 7% of the cost of full multi-vector retrieval. ArtSeek sets a new state of the art in artwork attribute classification, with +8.4% F1 in style classification over GraphCLIP, and gains +7.1 BLEU@1 on ArtPedia captioning. On five knowledge-grounded question answering benchmarks graded by an LLM judge from a different model family, retrieval helps where the answer is documented in our index or lies inside the classifier's label space (+0.19 mean correctness on two of them) and is neutral where it is not; what separates the two regimes is how much of a benchmark our knowledge base covers. The gain transfers to two non-Qwen backbones (+0.22 to +0.28). A blind human study and a qualitative analysis of successes and failures corroborate this picture and expose where retrieval still misleads. Data and code:this https URL
  </details>

- **[WildPPG: A Real-World PPG Dataset of Long Continuous Recordings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17540)**  `arXiv:2412.17540`  `cs.CV`  
  _Manuel Meier, Berken Utku Demirel, Christian Holz_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reflective photoplethysmography (PPG) has become the default sensing technique in wearable devices to monitor cardiac activity via a person's heart rate (HR). However, PPG-based HR estimates can be substantially impacted by factors such as the wearer's activities, sensor placement and resulting motion artifacts, as well as environmental characteristics such as temperature and ambient light. These and other factors can significantly impact and decrease HR prediction reliability. In this paper, we show that state-of-the-art HR estimation methods struggle when processing \emph{representative} data from everyday activities in outdoor environments, likely because they rely on existing datasets that captured controlled conditions. We introduce a novel multimodal dataset and benchmark results for continuous PPG recordings during outdoor activities from 16 participants over 13.5 hours, captured from four wearable sensors, each worn at a different location on the body, totaling 216\,hours. Our recordings include accelerometer, temperature, and altitude data, as well as a synchronized Lead I-based electrocardiogram for ground-truth HR references. Participants completed a round trip from Zurich to Jungfraujoch, a tall mountain in Switzerland over the course of one day. The trip included outdoor and indoor activities such as walking, hiking, stair climbing, eating, drinking, and resting at various temperatures and altitudes (up to 3,571\,m above sea level) as well as using cars, trains, cable cars, and lifts for transport -- all of which impacted participants' physiological dynamics. We also present a novel method that estimates HR values more robustly in such real-world scenarios than existing baselines.
  </details>

- **[Adaptive Replay Refinement: Allocating Test-Time Compute Between Revision and Resampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28294)**  `arXiv:2610.28294`  `cs.CL` `cs.LG`  
  _Ahsan Bilal, Muhammad Ahmed Mohsin, Muhammad Umer, Ayesha Mohsin, Sarmad Bilal, Dean F. Hougen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Test-time scaling allocates additional inference compute to refining existing solutions or generating new ones. This raises a fundamental question: Should I keep improving this solution, or stop working on it and try a completely new solution? Existing methods make this decision using fixed refinement schedules, self-verification judgments, or learned controllers, which can waste compute or rely on signals that may be incorrect or require additional training. We introduce Adaptive Replay Refinement (ARR), a training-free method that detects when a stationary revision reaches a fixed point and reallocates the released compute to a new independent sample; replay decoding treats the current solution as a draft and verifies unchanged tokens in parallel, enabling efficient fixed-point detection. Across seven models from five families and seven benchmarks, ARR matches majority voting on three of five mathematical reasoning benchmarks at equal compute, while reducing critical-path serial passes by up to 2.4 times. On MATH500, ARR achieves 84.6% accuracy with only 41% of the serial passes required by majority voting. These results demonstrate that detecting refinement exhaustion enables adaptive reallocation from revision to independent sampling, improving inference efficiency while highlighting the limits of self-revision. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[ProVe: Proposer-Verifier Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27815)**  `arXiv:2610.27815`  `cs.CL` `cs.LG`  
  _Ziqi Jin, Weiwen Xu, Jiaxi Li, Yujie Zhong, Qi She, Aixin Sun_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Speculative decoding accelerates autoregressive (AR) language models by letting a small model draft tokens serially and a large model verify them in parallel. We reverse these roles. Starting from a standard AR checkpoint, we fine-tune the large model into a proposer that predicts a block of future tokens in a single forward pass, then use one additional forward pass to verify the proposed block. This gives ProVe, Proposer-Verifier decoding. The verifier can be a smaller AR model or the proposer itself; the latter gives ProVe-lossless, which reproduces the proposer's greedy AR decoding. Block proposals already provide substantial room for acceleration: with a block size of 16, the proposed block has an average AR-matching prefix of 6.6 tokens on GSM8K. Across six benchmarks with a 32B proposer, ProVe-lossless reaches 2.5x average AR throughput without training a separate draft head. With a small 3B verifier, ProVe reaches 2.7x average throughput at 2.6 accuracy points below AR on the six-task average, providing a controllable tradeoff between speed and accuracy.
  </details>

- **[DStill: Trajectory Consistency Distillation for Accelerated One-Step Drafting in Block Diffusion Speculative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27379)**  `arXiv:2610.27379`  `cs.CL` `cs.LG`  
  _Shreyas Kulkarni, Uttam Gurram, Madhav Kanda, Krishna Teja Chitty Venkata, Shichu Chen, Kartik Balasubramaniam, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Block-diffusion speculative decoding accelerates large language model inference by drafting multiple tokens in parallel. However, one-step drafting in discrete diffusion is limited by token factorization error: each token is predicted from the same masked block, without conditioning on the tokens selected at other positions in that step. This can produce inconsistent drafts and reduce acceptance length. Iterative refinement mitigates this limitation by conditioning later predictions on previously committed tokens, though additional passes increase drafting cost. To address this trade-off between draft fidelity and inference latency, we introduce DStill, a two-stage framework that distills a target-conditioned drafter's own multi-step refinement into one-step drafting. We first train the drafter to complete partially revealed blocks, then use its refinement trajectories to train a student to predict the final completion in one pass. Using a curriculum that gradually transitions from partial to fully masked inputs, we successfully transfer the multi-step refinement capabilities into one step. Across reasoning, math, code benchmarks on three model families DStill improves acceptance length by up to 28% and speedup by up to 25% over one-step DFlash, achieving up to 6.45x speedup over auto-regressive decoding.
  </details>

- **[ReRef-3D: A Benchmark for Spatial Referring Expression-Guided 3D Object Placement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16011)**  `arXiv:2608.16011`  `cs.CL` `cs.CV`  
  _Mary Lynn Martin, Yifei Zhang, Martha Palmer, Maria Leonor Pacheco_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We introduce ReRef-3D, a benchmark for language-guided single-object placement in controlled 3D tabletop scenes. It contains 33,826 instructions across 998 CLEVR-derived scenes, spanning 16 placement types and direct, one-hop, and two-hop references. Given that an instruction defines a region of acceptable placements rather than one coordinate, predictions are evaluated by relation satisfaction and physical validity. Each instruction also includes a verified natural rephrasing. We evaluate LLaVA-3D, 3D-LLM, 3D-LLaVA, LL3DA, and PlaceIt3D, with PlaceIt3D evaluated under both point-based and region-based supervision. Region-supervised PlaceIt3D achieves the highest placement accuracy at about 80% and LLaVA-3D reaches about 70%. Performance varies by relation type, but nearest and aligned remain the most difficult. Rephrasing has little effect on performance overall.
  </details>

- **[TokenSwap: Benchmarking and Reducing the Modality Gap in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28640)**  `arXiv:2607.28640`  `cs.CL` `cs.CV`  
  _Andong Hua, Colton Bishop, Igor Mordatch, Arian Hosseini, Jindong Gu, Aleksandra Faust, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) should exhibit cross-modal consistency, generating consistent responses given semantically equivalent inputs across modalities. However, we observe a systematic discrepancy in model predictions under such cross-modal variations. Specifically, we define the modality gap as the difference in model performance under semantically equivalent textual and multimodal inputs. We introduce TokenSwap, a method that constructs such inputs by replacing textual concepts with semantically aligned images, resulting in sequences where visual tokens are interleaved with text tokens. Based on TokenSwap, we transform existing text-based benchmarks (e.g., MMLU) into image-interleaved counterparts, resulting in TokenSwap-Bench. Across 42 MLLMs, we observe a pervasive modality gap, with performance decreasing by 4.2% to 47.4% when moving from text-only to image-interleaved inputs, averaging 19.6% +/- 3.3% across models. Notably, reasoning models exhibit consistently smaller gaps, achieving an average gap of 10.1% compared to 25.5% for non-reasoning models, suggesting greater robustness in multimodal reasoning across equivalent textual and visual representations. In contrast, neither prompting strategies nor scaling training compute alone reliably reduces the modality gap. Finally, we demonstrate that incorporating TokenSwap during training effectively mitigates this gap, improving cross-modal alignment while preserving strong text-only and vision-language model performance. The project page is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[On-Policy Transition Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28048)**  `arXiv:2610.28048`  `cs.CL` `cs.AI` `cs.LG`  
  _Runyang You, Yongqi Li, Tiezheng Yu, Wenjie Li_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  On-policy distillation (OPD) consolidates expert capabilities into a single student by matching teacher and student final-layer distributions on student-generated rollouts. We investigate whether depthwise prediction transitions, the changes in predictions between layers, can provide additional task-relevant supervision. Our diagnostic study finds that stronger teacher--student agreement in intermediate transitions is associated with higher answer correctness within questions, with correct responses exhibiting lower mean transition KL across all measured intermediate layer pairs. These observations motivate On-Policy Transition Distillation (OPTD), which augments final-layer distribution matching by aligning teacher and student layer-to-layer log-probability increments on student-generated rollouts. Evaluations across three backbones and eight benchmarks spanning mathematics, coding, and instruction following show that OPTD achieves the best or tied-best overall performance in both dual- and three-domain distillation. In the three-domain setting, OPTD recovers $\approx 90\%$ of the overall teacher--student capability gap, compared with $\approx 75\%$ for OPD.
  </details>

- **[Benchmarking Arabic Dialects for Math Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28607)**  `arXiv:2610.28607`  `cs.CL` `cs.AI`  
  _Noor Abo Mokh, Teresa Lynn, Nizar Habash, Ahmed Elshabrawy, Sara Alhajeri, Alaa Elsetohy, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Mathematical reasoning remains underexplored for low-resource languages, including Dialectal Arabic. To bridge this gap, we introduce the DAR-MGSM (Dialectal Arabic Multilingual Grade School Math) Benchmark, the first manually curated benchmark for evaluating mathematical reasoning in Dialectal Arabic. DAR-MGSM consists of math word problems (MWPs) requiring multi-step reasoning and covers five major Arabic dialects alongside Modern Standard Arabic (MSA). The benchmark comprises two parallel versions: translated-only and translated & culturally localized. The latter version adapts entities and references to regional varieties. We evaluate eleven Arabic-centric and multilingual large language models on DAR-MGSM. The results show that the performance generally declines on the localized version, highlighting the challenges introduced by culturally grounded adaptations. However, the magnitude of this effect varies across models, with Arabic-centric models exhibiting different levels of sensitivity to localization. A mechanistic interpretability analysis shows that translated and localized variants of the same MWPs often induce distinct internal reasoning trajectories, even when aggregate accuracy remains similar. Overall, our results show that mathematical reasoning performance depends on both linguistic variety and cultural context.
  </details>

- **[Temporal Evidence-Parameter Co-Alignment for LLM Reasoning over Temporal Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28176)**  `arXiv:2610.28176`  `cs.CL` `cs.AI`  
  _Jiapu Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large Language Models (LLMs) often struggle with temporally grounded reasoning because their static parametric memory is misaligned with the dynamic evolution of real-world knowledge. Although RAG and Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) provide practical ways to enhance LLMs, they remain insufficient for temporal reasoning. Existing retrieval methods typically prioritize semantic relevance while overlooking temporal validity, event order, and evolutionary dependencies, resulting in evidence that is relevant in isolation but temporally fragmented. Meanwhile, conventional PEFT methods usually employ fixed and input-agnostic low-rank updates, making it difficult to capture time-dependent entity semantics and relation drift. To address these limitations, we propose Temporal Evidence-Parameter Co-Alignment (TEPCA), a framework for LLM reasoning over TKGs. TEPCA aligns LLM reasoning with temporal knowledge at both the evidence and parameter levels. At the evidence level, it introduces time-constrained Dynamic Relevance-Guided Path (DRGP) pruning to preserve globally coherent temporal paths, together with Mutual-Information (MI)-based pruning to retain query-informative local facts under temporal constraints. At the parameter level, it integrates Dynamic-Gated LoRA (DG-LoRA), a timestamp-conditioned low-rank adaptation mechanism that dynamically modulates the effective adaptation subspace according to temporal context. This design enables LLMs to better capture fine-grained entity evolution and time-dependent relation semantics while preserving parameter efficiency. Experimental results on two TKG benchmarks demonstrate that TEPCA achieves state-of-the-art performance. Additional evaluations of DG-LoRA on eight commonsense QA datasets further verify its general input-adaptive capacity, scalability across LLM backbones, and interpretability.
  </details>

- **[BAIT: Can LLMs Find What Biases a Reader? A Benchmark for Cognitive Bias Triggers in News](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28092)**  `arXiv:2610.28092`  `cs.CL` `cs.AI`  
  _Yousra Fettach, Tijl De Bie_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Persuasive writing exploits cognitive biases, recruiting a reader's mental shortcuts to shape belief, often without the reader noticing. We call the linguistic features exploiting such mental shortcuts cognitive bias triggers. As large language models (LLMs) increasingly mediate how people encounter news, summarizing it, ranking it, and answering questions over it, a natural question is whether they can recognize the features of a text that bias a human reader. We introduce BAIT (Bias Annotation In Text), a resource for detecting cognitive bias triggers in news. BAIT pairs a schema of nine triggers, each specified in an annotator codebook, with 50 English news articles annotated by trained annotators at the article and span levels and adjudicated into a gold standard. On this data we define two tasks, detecting which triggers a text contains and locating the spans that carry them. We then benchmark six frontier LLMs zero-shot and with three or ten in-context examples. The models approach human agreement on which triggers an article contains, but rarely recover its full set of triggers, and they reach less than half of human agreement in locating the passages that carry them.
